I wouldn’t say I’m suddenly needing less sleep or feeling unusually rested or anything.\n\nI may push through things, but I don’t think it’s because I don’t need sleep. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself talking more than usual or feeling like you can't stop talking, even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to, but I think that does happen.\n\nLike in a parent conference or a staff meeting, I’ll go in telling myself, “Okay, just be brief,” and then I’ll hear myself giving three examples, or telling some story from my classroom, and then adding ideas that nobody really asked for. And I can see the other person kind of trying to wrap it up, and I’m still talking.\n\nI don’t mean to take over, but… yeah, it’s hard for me to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts are racing or that your mind is going a hundred miles a minute?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly, like “racing thoughts.” I mean, my mind is usually busy, but I don’t know if it’s that hundred-miles-an-hour feeling you’re asking about.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed it in that way. And I don’t think people around me would say I’m acting unusually high or anything like that.\n\nI’m not sure if I’m answering that right, but… no, I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself doing things that are out of character for you — like spending a lot of money, taking risks, or acting impulsively — more than you usually would?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. leaves seat in situations when remaining seated is expected.\n  manifestation: When I’m supposed to sit through a training, dinner with the family, or a school meeting, I keep finding reasons to get up—refill water, check the copier, look in on my relative—even when I know it makes me look disrespectful.\n- key: S013\n  name: Quiet_Activity_Difficulty\n  description: Often unable to play or engage in leisure activities quietly.\n  manifestation: Quiet time at home has become almost impossible for me, because if my spouse is trying to watch a show or my relative is resting, I’ll start narrating what I’m doing, humming, or moving things around until someone asks me to please settle down.\n- key: S014\n  name: Constant_Activity\n  description: Is often 'on the go', acting as if 'driven by a motor'.\n  manifestation: What brings me in is that I feel like I’m constantly running on a motor lately, like I finish one thing at school or home and immediately jump into the next without ever being able to shut myself off.\n- key: S015\n  name: Excessive_Talking\n  description: Often talks excessively.\n  manifestation: I’ll go into a parent conference or a staff meeting meaning to be brief, and then I hear myself giving three examples, a story from my classroom, and ideas nobody asked for while the other person is clearly trying to wrap it up.\n- key: S018\n  name: Interrupting_Intruding\n  description: Often interrupts or intrudes on others (e.g., butts into conversations, games, or activities).\n  manifestation: I keep jumping into conversations between colleagues, parents, or my spouse and our relative because I think I’m helping, but I end up talking over them or taking charge before they’ve even had a chance to finish.\n- key: I might push through, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I don’t need sleep. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you didn’t really need to sleep, even if you only got a few hours and still felt alert or energized the next day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’m sorry, I know you’ve asked it a couple ways, but I don’t want to overstate anything.\n\nIf I only get a few hours, I might still get up and do what I have to do, but I wouldn’t say I feel alert or energized because of it. I usually feel like I missed sleep. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Even if you’re not feeling unusually rested, have you ever found yourself needing to sleep much less than usual and still being able to function well the next day?\n\nPatient: I guess… not in the way you mean, no. I guess what’s been bothering me is that lately I feel like I’m kind of always “on,” if that makes sense. Like I’ll finish something at school or at home, and instead of being able to slow down, I’m immediately jumping into the next thing.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s like I’m running on a motor and I can’t really shut myself off. That’s probably the best way I can put it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need less sleep lately, or that you don’t feel as tired as you used to, even when you’re going non-stop?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, no. I mean, I get tired like anyone else. I wouldn’t say I’m suddenly needing less sleep or feeling unusually rested or anything.\n\nI may push through things, but I don’t think it’s because I don’t need sleep. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself talking more than usual or feeling like you can't stop talking, even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to, but I think that does happen.\n\nLike in a parent conference or a staff meeting, I’ll go in telling myself, “Okay, just be brief,” and then I’ll hear myself giving three examples, or telling some story from my classroom, and then adding ideas that nobody really asked for. And I can see the other person kind of trying to wrap it up, and I’m still talking.\n\nI don’t mean to take over, but… yeah, it’s hard for me to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts are racing or that your mind is going a hundred miles a minute?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly, like “racing thoughts.” I mean, my mind is usually busy, but I don’t know if it’s that hundred-miles-an-hour feeling you’re asking about.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed it in that way. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing more activities than usual, like taking on extra projects, making a lot of plans, or feeling driven to keep moving or doing things even when you don’t have to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s actually a big part of why I’m here.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but lately I do feel like I’m just constantly going. Like I finish something at school, and instead of taking a breath I’m immediately onto the next thing. Same at home — I’ll get one thing done and then just jump into another task.\n\nIt’s not even always that I have to. It’s more like I can’t quite shut myself off, if that makes sense. Like I’m running on a motor. I know that probably sounds a little much, but that’s the best way I can describe it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually upbeat, overly confident, or irritable — like you were on top of the world or had more energy than usual, even if others around you didn’t seem to notice or share that feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI can get kind of upbeat if something’s going well, or I might feel energized when I’m busy, but I wouldn’t say I feel “on top of the world” or overly confident. And I don’t think people around me would say I’m acting unusually high or anything like that.\n\nI’m not sure if I’m answering that right, but… no, I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself doing things that are out of character for you — like spending a lot of money, taking risks, or acting impulsively — more than you usually would?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. But there are times I catch myself drifting or having to pull myself back. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you felt rested even after only a few hours of sleep, or when you felt like you didn’t really need much sleep at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no.     "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF31.2, F43.0, F43.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what's been bothering you or what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Sure… um, thanks for seeing me. I guess what’s been bothering me is that lately I feel like I’m kind of always “on,” if that makes sense. Like I’ll finish something at school or at home, and instead of being able to slow down, I’m immediately jumping into the next thing.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s like I’m running on a motor and I can’t really shut myself off. That’s probably the best way I can put it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need less sleep lately, or that you don’t feel as tired as you used to, even when you’re going non-stop?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, no. I mean, I get tired like anyone else. I wouldn’t say I’m suddenly needing less sleep or feeling unusually rested or anything.\n\nI may push through things, but I don’t think it’s because I don’t need sleep. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself talking more than usual or feeling like you can't stop talking, even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to, but I think that does happen.\n\nLike in a parent conference or a staff meeting, I’ll go in telling myself, “Okay, just be brief,” and then I’ll hear myself giving three examples, or telling some story from my classroom, and then adding ideas that nobody really asked for. And I can see the other person kind of trying to wrap it up, and I’m still talking.\n\nI don’t mean to take over, but… yeah, it’s hard for me to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts are racing or that your mind is going a hundred miles a minute?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly, like “racing thoughts.” I mean, my mind is usually busy, but I don’t know if it’s that hundred-miles-an-hour feeling you’re asking about.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed it in that way. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing more activities than usual, like taking on extra projects, making a lot of plans, or feeling driven to keep moving or doing things even when you don’t have to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s actually a big part of why I’m here.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but lately I do feel like I’m just constantly going. Like I finish something at school, and instead of taking a breath I’m immediately onto the next thing. Same at home — I’ll get one thing done and then just jump into another task.\n\nIt’s not even always that I have to. It’s more like I can’t quite shut myself off, if that makes sense. Like I’m running on a motor. I know that probably sounds a little much, but that’s the best way I can describe it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually upbeat, overly confident, or irritable — like you were on top of the world or had more energy than usual, even if others around you didn’t seem to notice or share that feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI can get kind of upbeat if something’s going well, or I might feel energized when I’m busy, but I wouldn’t say I feel “on top of the world” or overly confident. And I don’t think people around me would say I’m acting unusually high or anything like that.\n\nI’m not sure if I’m answering that right, but… no, I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself doing things that are out of character for you — like spending a lot of money, taking risks, or acting impulsively — more than you usually would?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I can be a little impulsive in small ways, like deciding to do something quickly, but not like spending a bunch of money or taking risks that would be really out of character.\n\nAt least, nothing comes to mind that I’d be worried about. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these periods of feeling constantly 'on' or driven to keep doing things come and go, or has it been more of a steady, ongoing pattern for you?\n\nPatient: It’s been more steady, I think. Not like a few days here and there where it suddenly switches on and then goes away.\n\nI’d say this level of it has been going on for… maybe about eight months now. It seemed to get a lot harder after our older relative moved in with us. I don’t want to blame that, exactly, but that’s around when I really noticed I wasn’t settling down the way I used to.\n\nDoes that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're more easily distracted than usual, or that it's harder to stay focused on tasks, even when you're trying to pay attention?\n\nPatient: I’m… not completely sure. I mean, I do get distracted, sure, but I don’t know if it’s more than usual or just normal stress and having a lot going on.\n\nI try to pay attention, especially when something matters, and I’d like to think I can still manage it. But there are times I catch myself drifting or having to pull myself back. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you felt rested even after only a few hours of sleep, or when you felt like you didn’t really need much sleep at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I have nights where I don’t sleep as much as I’d like, but I wouldn’t say I wake up feeling great or like I don’t need sleep.\n\nIf anything, I usually still feel like I could use more. I haven’t really had that “running fine on just a few hours” kind of thing, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you didn’t really need much sleep, even if you only got a few hours and still felt rested the next day?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I know I sort of answered that already, but… no, I haven’t had that where I only sleep a few hours and then feel fine or rested the next day.\n\nIf I don’t sleep much, I usually notice it. I might push through, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I don’t need sleep. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you didn’t really need to sleep, even if you only got a few hours and still felt alert or energized the next day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’m sorry, I know you’ve asked it a couple ways, but I don’t want to overstate anything.\n\nIf I only get a few hours, I might still get up and do what I have to do, but I wouldn’t say I feel alert or energized because of it. I usually feel like I missed sleep. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Even if you’re not feeling unusually rested, have you ever found yourself needing to sleep much less than usual and still being able to function well the next day?\n\nPatient: I guess… not in the way you mean, no. I’ve had days after a short night where I still managed to get through work or whatever I had to do, but I wouldn’t say I functioned well because I needed less sleep.\n\nIt’s more like I just pushed through it. I mean, I can keep going if I have to, like anyone can, but it’s not like I’m running fine on two or three hours or anything.\n\nI usually feel it somehow, even if I try not to make a big deal out of it. So… I’d say I haven’t noticed that, unless I’m misunderstanding what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually good — like really happy, excited, or even irritable — in a way that was different from your usual mood, and that lasted for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I have good days and bad days like anyone, and I can get excited about things, sure. I guess what’s been bothering me is that lately I feel like I’m kind of always “on,” if that makes sense. Like I’ll finish something at school or at home, and instead of being able to slow down, I’m immediately jumping into the next thing.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s like I’m running on a motor and I can’t really shut myself off. That’s probably the best way I can put it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need less sleep lately, or that you don’t feel as tired as you used to, even when you’re going non-stop?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, no. I mean, I get tired like anyone else. I wouldn’t say I’m suddenly needing less sleep or feeling unusually rested or anything.\n\nI may push through things, but I don’t think it’s because I don’t need sleep. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself talking more than usual or feeling like you can't stop talking, even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to, but I think that does happen.\n\nLike in a parent conference or a staff meeting, I’ll go in telling myself, “Okay, just be brief,” and then I’ll hear myself giving three examples, or telling some story from my classroom, and then adding ideas that nobody really asked for. And I can see the other person kind of trying to wrap it up, and I’m still talking.\n\nI don’t mean to take over, but… yeah, it’s hard for me to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts are racing or that your mind is going a hundred miles a minute?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly, like “racing thoughts.” I mean, my mind is usually busy, but I don’t know if it’s that hundred-miles-an-hour feeling you’re asking about.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed it in that way. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing more activities than usual, like taking on extra projects, making a lot of plans, or feeling driven to keep moving or doing things even when you don’t have to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s actually a big part of why I’m here.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but lately I do feel like I’m just constantly going. Like I finish something at school, and instead of taking a breath I’m immediately onto the next thing. Same at home — I’ll get one thing done and then just jump into another task.\n\nIt’s not even always that I have to. It’s more like I can’t quite shut myself off, if that makes sense. Like I’m running on a motor. I know that probably sounds a little much, but that’s the best way I can describe it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually upbeat, overly confident, or irritable — like you were on top of the world or had more energy than usual, even if others around you didn’t seem to notice or share that feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI can get kind of upbeat if something’s going well, or I might feel energized when I’m busy, but I wouldn’t say I feel “on top of the world” or overly confident. And I don’t think people around me would say I’m acting unusually high or anything like that.\n\nI’m not sure if I’m answering that right, but… no, I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself doing things that are out of character for you — like spending a lot of money, taking risks, or acting impulsively — more than you usually would?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. But there are times I catch myself drifting or having to pull myself back. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you felt rested even after only a few hours of sleep, or when you felt like you didn’t really need much sleep at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I have nights where I don’t sleep as much as I’d like, but I wouldn’t say I wake up feeling great or like I don’t need sleep.\n\nIf anything, I usually still feel like I could use more. I haven’t really had that “running fine on just a few hours” kind of thing, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you didn’t really need much sleep, even if you only got a few hours and still felt rested the next day?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I know I sort of answered that already, but… no, I haven’t had that where I only sleep a few hours and then feel fine or rested the next day.\n\nIf I don’t sleep much, I usually notice it. I might push through, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I don’t need sleep. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you didn’t really need to sleep, even if you only got a few hours and still felt alert or energized the next day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’m sorry, I know you’ve asked it a couple ways, but I don’t want to overstate anything.\n\nIf I only get a few hours, I might still get up and do what I have to do, but I wouldn’t say I feel alert or energized because of it. I usually feel like I missed sleep. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Even if you’re not feeling unusually rested, have you ever found yourself needing to sleep much less than usual and still being able to function well the next day?\n\nPatient: I guess… not in the way you mean, no. I’ve had days after a short night where I still managed to get through work or whatever I had to do, but I wouldn’t say I functioned well because I needed less sleep.\n\nIt’s more like I just pushed through it. I don’t really remember a stretch where I was sleeping much less and thinking, “Oh, I’m fine like this.” Is that the distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Even if you don’t feel rested, have you ever found yourself not needing as much sleep as usual—like only sleeping a few hours and still being able to keep going without much trouble?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I can keep going if I have to, like anyone can, but it’s not like I’m running fine on two or three hours or anything.\n\nI usually feel it somehow, even if I try not to make a big deal out of it. So… I’d say I haven’t noticed that, unless I’m misunderstanding what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually good — like really happy, excited, or even irritable — in a way that was different from your usual mood, and that lasted for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I have good days and bad days like anyone, and I can get excited about things, sure. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself talking more than usual or feeling like you can't stop talking, even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to, but I think that does happen.\n\nLike in a parent conference or a staff meeting, I’ll go in telling myself, “Okay, just be brief,” and then I’ll hear myself giving three examples, or telling some story from my classroom, and then adding ideas that nobody really asked for. And I can see the other person kind of trying to wrap it up, and I’m still talking.\n\nI don’t mean to take over, but… yeah, it’s hard for me to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts are racing or that your mind is going a hundred miles a minute?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly, like “racing thoughts.” I mean, my mind is usually busy, but I don’t know if it’s that hundred-miles-an-hour feeling you’re asking about.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed it in that way. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing more activities than usual, like taking on extra projects, making a lot of plans, or feeling driven to keep moving or doing things even when you don’t have to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s actually a big part of why I’m here.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but lately I do feel like I’m just constantly going. Like I finish something at school, and instead of taking a breath I’m immediately onto the next thing. Same at home — I’ll get one thing done and then just jump into another task.\n\nIt’s not even always that I have to. It’s more like I can’t quite shut myself off, if that makes sense. Like I’m running on a motor. I know that probably sounds a little much, but that’s the best way I can describe it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually upbeat, overly confident, or irritable — like you were on top of the world or had more energy than usual, even if others around you didn’t seem to notice or share that feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI can get kind of upbeat if something’s going well, or I might feel energized when I’m busy, but I wouldn’t say I feel “on top of the world” or overly confident. And I don’t think people around me would say I’m acting unusually high or anything like that.\n\nI’m not sure if I’m answering that right, but… no, I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself doing things that are out of character for you — like spending a lot of money, taking risks, or acting impulsively — more than you usually would?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I can be a little impulsive in small ways, like deciding to do something quickly, but not like spending a bunch of money or taking risks that would be really out of character.\n\nAt least, nothing comes to mind that I’d be worried about. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these periods of feeling constantly 'on' or driven to keep doing things come and go, or has it been more of a steady, ongoing pattern for you?\n\nPatient: It’s been more steady, I think. Not like a few days here and there where it suddenly switches on and then goes away.\n\nI’d say this level of it has been going on for… maybe about eight months now. It seemed to get a lot harder after our older relative moved in with us. I don’t want to blame that, exactly, but that’s around when I really noticed I wasn’t settling down the way I used to.\n\nDoes that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're more easily distracted than usual, or that it's harder to stay focused on tasks, even when you're trying to pay attention?\n\nPatient: I’m… not completely sure. I mean, I do get distracted, sure, but I don’t know if it’s more than usual or just normal stress and having a lot going on.\n\nI try to pay attention, especially when something matters, and I’d like to think I can still manage it. But there are times I catch myself drifting or having to pull myself back. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you felt rested even after only a few hours of sleep, or when you felt like you didn’t really need much sleep at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I have nights where I don’t sleep as much as I’d like, but I wouldn’t say I wake up feeling great or like I don’t need sleep.\n\nIf anything, I usually still feel like I could use more. I haven’t really had that “running fine on just a few hours” kind of thing, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you didn’t really need much sleep, even if you only got a few hours and still felt rested the next day?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I know I sort of answered that already, but… no, I haven’t had that where I only sleep a few hours and then feel fine or rested the next day.\n\nIf I don’t sleep much, I usually notice it. I might push through, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I don’t need sleep. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you didn’t really need to sleep, even if you only got a few hours and still felt alert or energized the next day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’m sorry, I know you’ve asked it a couple ways, but I don’t want to overstate anything.\n\nIf I only get a few hours, I might still get up and do what I have to do, but I wouldn’t say I feel alert or energized because of it. I usually feel like I missed sleep. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Even if you’re not feeling unusually rested, have you ever found yourself needing to sleep much less than usual and still being able to function well the next day?\n\nPatient: I guess… not in the way you mean, no. I’ve had days after a short night where I still managed to get through work or whatever I had to do, but I wouldn’t say I functioned well because I needed less sleep.\n\nIt’s more like I just pushed through it. I don’t really remember a stretch where I was sleeping much less and thinking, “Oh, I’m fine like this.” Is that the distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Even if you don’t feel rested, have you ever found yourself not needing as much sleep as usual—like only sleeping a few hours and still being able to keep going without much trouble?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I can keep going if I have to, like anyone can, but it’s not like I’m running fine on two or three hours or anything.\n\nI usually feel it somehow, even if I try not to make a big deal out of it. So… I’d say I haven’t noticed that, unless I’m misunderstanding what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually good — like really happy, excited, or even irritable — in a way that was different from your usual mood, and that lasted for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I have good days and bad days like anyone, and I can get excited about things, sure. But not several days where I felt unusually happy or wired or irritable in a way that seemed really different from me.\n\nAt least nothing that stands out to me as, you know, “that was a whole episode.” Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed that you don't really need to sleep much — like only a few hours — and even without that much sleep, you still feel like you can keep going without much trouble?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I know I’ve had nights where I didn’t sleep much and still had to keep going the next day, but I wouldn’t say I felt like I didn’t need the sleep.\n\nI usually feel tired or kind of off if I don’t get enough. So I don’t think I’ve had that thing where a few hours is enough and I’m just fine. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed that you don’t really need much sleep — like only sleeping a few hours — and even without that much sleep, you still feel like you can keep going without much trouble?\n\nPatient: I don’t think that really happens for me. I mean, if I only sleep a few hours, I can still get through the day if I have to, but I wouldn’t say I feel fine or like I didn’t need the sleep.\n\nI usually notice it somehow—tired, slower, something like that. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself talking more than usual or feeling like you can't stop talking, even if you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to, but I think that does happen.\n\nLike in a parent conference or a staff meeting, I’ll go in telling myself, “Okay, just be brief,” and then I’ll hear myself giving three examples, or telling some story from my classroom, and then adding ideas that nobody really asked for. And I can see the other person kind of trying to wrap it up, and I’m still talking.\n\nI don’t mean to take over, but… yeah, it’s hard for me to stop once I get going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts are racing or that your mind is going a hundred miles a minute?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that strongly, like “racing thoughts.” I mean, my mind is usually busy, but I don’t know if it’s that hundred-miles-an-hour feeling you’re asking about.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t really noticed it in that way. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing more activities than usual, like taking on extra projects, making a lot of plans, or feeling driven to keep moving or doing things even when you don’t have to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s actually a big part of why I’m here.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but lately I do feel like I’m just constantly going. Like I finish something at school, and instead of taking a breath I’m immediately onto the next thing. Same at home — I’ll get one thing done and then just jump into another task.\n\nIt’s not even always that I have to. It’s more like I can’t quite shut myself off, if that makes sense. Like I’m running on a motor. I know that probably sounds a little much, but that’s the best way I can describe it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually upbeat, overly confident, or irritable — like you were on top of the world or had more energy than usual, even if others around you didn’t seem to notice or share that feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you’re describing.\n\nI can get kind of upbeat if something’s going well, or I might feel energized when I’m busy, but I wouldn’t say I feel “on top of the world” or overly confident. And I don’t think people around me would say I’m acting unusually high or anything like that.\n\nI’m not sure if I’m answering that right, but… no, I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself doing things that are out of character for you — like spending a lot of money, taking risks, or acting impulsively — more than you usually would?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I can be a little impulsive in small ways, like deciding to do something quickly, but not like spending a bunch of money or taking risks that would be really out of character.\n\nAt least, nothing comes to mind that I’d be worried about. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these periods of feeling constantly 'on' or driven to keep doing things come and go, or has it been more of a steady, ongoing pattern for you?\n\nPatient: It’s been more steady, I think. Not like a few days here and there where it suddenly switches on and then goes away.\n\nI’d say this level of it has been going on for… maybe about eight months now. It seemed to get a lot harder after our older relative moved in with us. I don’t want to blame that, exactly, but that’s around when I really noticed I wasn’t settling down the way I used to.\n\nDoes that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're more easily distracted than usual, or that it's harder to stay focused on tasks, even when you're trying to pay attention?\n\nPatient: I’m… not completely sure. I mean, I do get distracted, sure, but I don’t know if it’s more than usual or just normal stress and having a lot going on.\n\nI try to pay attention, especially when something matters, and I’d like to think I can still manage it. But there are times I catch myself drifting or having to pull myself back. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you felt rested even after only a few hours of sleep, or when you felt like you didn’t really need much sleep at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I have nights where I don’t sleep as much as I’d like, but I wouldn’t say I wake up feeling great or like I don’t need sleep.\n\nIf anything, I usually still feel like I could use more. I haven’t really had that “running fine on just a few hours” kind of thing, at least not that I’ve noticed. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you didn’t really need much sleep, even if you only got a few hours and still felt rested the next day?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I know I sort of answered that already, but… no, I haven’t had that where I only sleep a few hours and then feel fine or rested the next day.\n\nIf I don’t sleep much, I usually notice it. I might push through, but I wouldn’t say I feel like I don’t need sleep. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you didn’t really need to sleep, even if you only got a few hours and still felt alert or energized the next day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’m sorry, I know you’ve asked it a couple ways, but I don’t want to overstate anything.\n\nIf I only get a few hours, I might still get up and do what I have to do, but I wouldn’t say I feel alert or energized because of it. I usually feel like I missed sleep. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Even if you’re not feeling unusually rested, have you ever found yourself needing to sleep much less than usual and still being able to function well the next day?\n\nPatient: I guess… not in the way you mean, no. I’ve had days after a short night where I still managed to get through work or whatever I had to do, but I wouldn’t say I functioned well because I needed less sleep.\n\nIt’s more like I just pushed through it. I don’t really remember a stretch where I was sleeping much less and thinking, “Oh, I’m fine like this.” Is that the distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Even if you don’t feel rested, have you ever found yourself not needing as much sleep as usual—like only sleeping a few hours and still being able to keep going without much trouble?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I can keep going if I have to, like anyone can, but it’s not like I’m running fine on two or three hours or anything.\n\nI usually feel it somehow, even if I try not to make a big deal out of it. So… I’d say I haven’t noticed that, unless I’m misunderstanding what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually good — like really happy, excited, or even irritable — in a way that was different from your usual mood, and that lasted for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I have good days and bad days like anyone, and I can get excited about things, sure. But not several days where I felt unusually happy or wired or irritable in a way that seemed really different from me.\n\nAt least nothing that stands out to me as, you know, “that was a whole episode.” Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed that you don't really need to sleep much — like only a few hours — and even without that much sleep, you still feel like you can keep going without much trouble?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. So I don’t think I’ve had that thing where a few hours is enough and I’m just fine. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed that you don’t really need much sleep — like only sleeping a few hours — and even without that much sleep, you still feel like you can keep going without much trouble?\n\nPatient: I don’t think that really happens for me. I mean, if I only sleep a few hours, I can still get through the day if I have to, but I wouldn’t say I feel fine or like I didn’t need the sleep.\n\nI usually notice it somehow—tired, slower, something like that. So… no, I don’t think I’ve had that. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt down or really low for most of the day, lost interest in things you usually enjoy, or had other symptoms of depression that lasted for several days or more?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no. I mean, I’ve had days where I feel worn down or discouraged, like anybody does, especially when things pile up. But I don’t think it’s been most of the day for several days in a row, or where I really stopped caring about things I normally enjoy.\n\nAt least, nothing that feels like a clear depression episode to me. Is that the kind of distinction you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like you were unusually energetic, restless, or in a really good or irritable mood for most of the day, nearly every day for at least four days in a row, and it was different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in that kind of stretched-out way.
